ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u3	Good morning. The bill was entered into Parliament's Register on September 3, 2021. The Bureau of Parliament, at its meeting on 7 September 2021, ordered its publication in the Official Bulletin of the Parliament of Galicia and the opening of the deadline for the submission of amendments. Even so, and hearing the Speaker's Board, he agreed to the 3rd Commission on Economics, Farms and Budgets. The parliamentary groups did not present amendments to the whole and, speaking of the partial amendments, the Nationalist Block Galego made fifty-three amendments, and the Parliamentary Group of the Socialists of Galicia, forty-four, which were qualified on October 18, 2021. The 3rd Commission on Economics, Farms and Budgets, at its meeting on October 29, 2021, appointed the presentation. At the meetings of 19 and 23 November 2021 the presentation studied the articulated text of the project together with the amendments submitted, and completed its report, which contains the recommendations it made to the Commission, consisting of the adoption of Amendments Nos 2, 3, 4, 7, 11 and 13 of the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block of Wales, and paragraphs 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 33 of the Socialist Parliamentary Group. The approval of seven transactional amendments was also recommended. The Commission, meeting on 29 November 2021, adopted the motion for a resolution by a majority. Finally, the text of the statement that the debate and voting in the plenary shall be submitted at this session shall consist of an explanatory statement, followed by twenty-five articles, an additional provision, a transitional provision, a derogation provision, two finals and three annexes. Thank you for the work of the Judiciary Services and parliamentary groups, and thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u4	Thank you, Mrs. Presas. [[One person from the House's cleaning services comes to disinfect the floor.]] We begin the round of parliamentary groups to set their position on the dictatorship and, if so, defend the amendments maintained. Socialist Parliamentary Group, Mrs Rodríguez Rumbo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u5	Thank you, Mr. President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The importance of statistics is already covered by the Galician Autonomy Statute itself, where it is included as an exclusive competence of the Autonomous Community. In 1988 — i.e. thirty-three years ago — the Galicia Statistics Act was adopted, a law which already includes in its Article 2 what is meant by statistical activity, and which says that it is the compilation, processing and systematic management of quantifiable information; the publication and dissemination of the results necessary or useful for quantitative knowledge; the analysis of the demographic, agricultural, fisheries, industrial, commercial, financial, services, social, cultural and environmental conditions of Galicia; and, in general, any matter relating to the living conditions, purposes and competences of the Autonomous Community. Since statistics is then a useful tool for citizenship as a whole, which provides quantitative information to be able to exercise our rights, having an understanding of the socio-economic reality of our territory, facilitating comparison, enhancing the creation of knowledge, guiding the generation of wealth and well-being and favoring decision-making. Since the adoption of the Act in 1988, five Greek statistical plans have been approved, the first quarter and the next five years. These plans are the tool for managing and planning statistical activity in the period under review and for establishing the objectives, strategies and actions for the production of the statistics required in the population set in order to reach the most objective and complete possible knowledge of the socio-economic reality of our Community. For the development and materialization of these plans, our Community has the Galego Institute of Statistics, an autonomous body which exercises the direction and coordination of the statistical activity in Galicia. And we also count on the Galego Statistical Council, the highest consultative body in the statistical activity of the Autonomous Community to ensure the participation of social agents. The Socialists very much appreciate the work done by the Galego Statistical Council and the work done by the Galego Statistical Institute. For us, the data published by the IGE are one of the main sources used to develop our daily work; data that we use in virtually all parliamentary debates as support for our arguments, data that in many cases paradoxically are questioned or transformed by the German Government itself or by the People's Party Parliamentary Group, something that should not happen, since the data is data and therefore is not questionable. For example, we socialists use the statistical data published by the IGE when we ensure that the demographic decline in our country has been one of the most worrying issues for many years. Look, IGE data tells us that since 2009 we've lost more than 101,000 people in our community and we have 2021 277,621 people under 40 years of age than we had in 2009. I mean, if we made a division and made an average exactly the same for every year, we would be losing the order of 8,500 people a year. And according to the latest data also published by the IGE for 2021 of the inhabitants we have in our country, because we have 15.67% under 20 years of age, while 25.76% are people over 65; that is, we have ten points over 65 years of age compared to those under 20. These are quantitative data and are therefore not questionable. And what is the conclusion? That in twelve years of government Feijóo nothing has been done to reverse this situation. Quantitative data is needed to draw conclusions but also to search for solutions, but the German Government takes a lot more time to deny data than to search for solutions. If in the light of this data there were effective public policies that would prevent the emigration of our youth, so that those who left would return or make our country attractive so that they would come from outside, because the results would surely be different. The data initially serves to diagnose us and then they become used to evaluate the policies applied. Look, the German Government itself already recognized in its own Strategic Plan 2014-2020 that Galicia must create attractive living conditions for the population as the only way to cope with solutions to poor fertility or aging or emigration of youth to more dynamic territories, without a year after completion of this plan having taken important steps to reverse this situation in view of quantitative data. In this sense, it seems to us that taking into account our amendment, the amendment tabled by the Socialists, Amendment No 34, since knowing the reality of living in our country is the first step in implementing measures that facilitate access to it, essentially for the younger population, one of the most important measures after the quality employment for the settlement. So our amendment 34 in which, in section 2.7, of housing, we ask for a more comprehensive study of the reality of our country's housing, with goals like occupied, empty housing park, in property and rent and first-living evictions. Finally, it was negotiated with another amendment tabled by the Group of the Galician Nationalist Block and where it reached the following goal: housing park, equipment and characteristics; question that we thank and that we understand that this text improves a lot. In the same sense of the fight against depopulation, the Socialists consider it a shame that the Group of the People's Party did not allow our amendment 41 to prosper, since we understand that the current Strategic Plan 2015-2020 already sets the transversal objective, territorial cohesion and the fixing of the population in rural areas. So we ask that this amendment set itself a goal to affect the knowledge of the quantitative statistics set out in the measurements of each of the strategic objectives; important data for the knowledge of the results to be applied to these measures. We asked Amendment No 41 to add a new area, called Plans and Programs of the Galician Union, where three sections would be included. One first, with the Galician Statistical Plan 2015-2020; one second, with the Galician Strategic Plan 2021-2030, which the Welsh Government has announced will be approved shortly, we understand that in the 2022 exercise; and one last, in which the plans and programs of the Galician Union would be established. And, therefore, we would be aware of the entire population as a whole of the result of those measures that are established for each of the objectives set out in the various strategic plans. Today when we talk about population it's unthinkable not to make a gender breakdown. But today it is also necessary to make a breakdown of rural and urban life, since reality tells us that, above all, when we talk about women, there are substantial differences in living conditions or working conditions between women living in rural areas and women living in urban areas, so it is a pity that the majority group of the House has an amendment tabled by our group in which we simply ask to add a new paragraph in the exhibition of reasons and that it also places the introduction of the variable "greed of urbanization" in all studies carried out on women. Well, the bill of the Greek Statistical Plan 2022-2026 presented to us by the Government of Greece consists of twenty-five articles, a single additional provision, a transitional provision, a derogation provision and two last provisions, with three attachments, a first that is the thematic structure, a second of goals and information and a third of consolidated statistical operations and activities. Well, we socialists present forty-four amendments, eight to the articulated and thirty-six to the annexes, of which six were accepted and five dealt, that is, 25% of those presented. We are grateful for its inclusion, since we understand that the amendments tabled by the Socialists improve the law, give it greater transparency, annual information in the Greek Parliament of the country's statistical activity, it is established that the statistical secret goes beyond the end of the agreements, agreements or contracts, as well as the inclusion of a series of information goals that we socialists consider necessary. For example, in Amendment No 33, section 2.6, of culture, sport and leisure, we ask you to incorporate the goal of sports equipment and resources, something that was not included and we welcome that it is accepted by the majority group of this chamber. We also tabled Amendment 35, which was also accepted by the majority group, which is in section 3.1 of the agricultural sector, so with a transaction, it adds "sacrifice of livestock and other productions and animal yields." Amendment 38 to section 3.6 was also accepted, public administrations and other non-market services, with a transaction that ultimately remains with "resources and expenses of the Greek public administrations", a topic that also for the socialists is of fundamental importance to their knowledge. Or Amendment 39, section 3.7, markets and prices, which are established, thanks to our approach, "prices in the productive sectors." However, we have to regret that amendments were rejected that from our point of view were also important and that we do not understand why they were not incorporated. A group of amendments that were presented by the Socialist Group simply set a general goal for each of the sections, which we believe would enrich those attachments, setting out what is the general objective for each of them. Another amendment to which we expressly request in section 3.1 the occupation in the primary sector broken down by sex and the generational relief. It is true that it has been negotiated, but it seems to us of vital importance that this issue should be taken into account when developing these statistical goals. We also find it important and we are also sorry that Amendment No 29, which is section 1.1, of territory and natural resources, did not take into account, in which Socialists are asking for something that we understand fundamental also in the matter of population and also in the subject of population settlement, and it is to establish a map of services in the territory of Greece. Amendment 32 to section 2.5 of social conditions was also rejected, in which we ask that in the case of Risga if you had a statistic on approved applications, accepted, denied, waiting lists, the number of perceivers and the conditions of life. Amendment 37 was also rejected, section 3.3, of energy and industry, where we asked for two targets: renewable and non-renewable energies, and fuel prices. Amendment 38 to section 3.5 of the market services was also rejected, in which we are asking for goals that seem very important to us, essentially after having experienced the situation of the pandemic of the Covid-19, and which has mainly affected trade. We call for targets in retail trade, small trade and large areas, proximity trade, rural tourism and also the inclusion of banking commissions, credits granted, branches of financial institutions in Galicia and indicators of accessibility of the population to financial services. And finally, Amendment 42, in Section 4.2, concerning science and technology, on which we asked for public spending targets to be introduced in R & D+i, the number of patents, the investigator's staff and R & D+i in small and medium-sized enterprises. Companies are changing and statistics need to change according to these new realities, adapt to new moments to continue to be that useful and fundamental instrument for citizenship. For my part on this shift, nothing more and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u7	Thank you, Mr. President. Good day again to all and to all. Welcome also to the Government of Greece for this processing of this sixth plan. Well, in his day, American sociologist and writer Alvin Toffler, author of Future Shock, said that he anticipated and thought as no one about the technological changes of the second half of the 20th century and how his brutal impact on society would be that knowledge is the most democratic source of power. By making this reflection, it is necessary to point out, in the thread of the process of this new plan, the importance of this scientific discipline for Greek society, the importance of statistics as an instrument of self-government and also as an instrument, ultimately, democratic. Because knowing more and better reality strengthens the exercise of democracy, both by giving more capacity for correctness and attinuity to governments and institutions of all kinds, as well as by giving more knowledge to the citizenry to judge and demand those institutions what they should do. So for the Galego Nationalist Block, this new plan, its processing, must suppose a leap, a step further in the maturation of a Welsh statistic that is self-centered and designed to be useful to the Greek people. Perhaps between the emergency marabunta that has to address this parliament within the framework of a pandemic and an unprecedented economic crisis because of its characteristics, the processing of a legislative text that at least 75% are procedural and methodological issues may seem of lesser importance. On the contrary, however, at this very moment in which we process this text, we think that the context reveals the need for a stronger statistical tool. First, because of the importance of developing a self-government tool that we should take care of; secondly, because we are in a context of changes that also requires us to meet new needs and new working rhythms. I would like to remind you that for 40 years now Article 27 of the Statute of Self-Government gives our country exclusive jurisdiction over the organisation of self-government institutions and the statistics for the purposes of the Community. It's been thirty-eight years since the first decree that created the Galician Statistical Information Centre and thirty-three that this parliament approved its first statistical law. This is important to remember because it shows that we have already gone a path that is not smaller and, therefore, brought into the current context, shows how it would also play a greater leap, because Galicia is strategic to have good statistical information, it's a strategic issue as a country. That's why we don't present at the moment a refund amendment, because we understand that because of its characteristics this law has an important part of method and procedure, which has already been improved throughout our history, which is a consolidated, professional work and which has also an important consensus not only technical, but also political. Besides, the slow pace of the pandemic months makes approval very adjusted over time, because we're already in mid-December. But, in any case, we have made numerous moves in the process of partial amendments, seeking a very clear objective, which is a qualitative leap in the statistical process of our country. In this spirit, in the spirit of seeking a stronger, more useful statistic and a greater transfer of knowledge to society and public policies, this parliamentary group has presented a total of fifty-three partial amendments (late to the joint and thirty-six to the annexes). They accepted, as already remembered, seven to the articulated and we also reached four transactions in the attachments. We would like to thank the majority parliamentary group, and there are aspects we understand that enrich the text or correct the absence. That congratulates us. But we also have to say that it seems insufficient and that the qualitative leap that would correspond to this moment in which — I insist — there is already a ripening of the statistical work of more than thirty years and there is a historic moment that demands from us also the acceleration of the responses that are given to the profound transformations that go fast, that affect the economic and social environment and that require rethinking what instruments we have, how we employ them and how we update them. So I'd like to make a comment on the amendments. The acceptance of paragraph 1 in Article 4 seems to be important to us. We were concerned about the loss of the role of this parliament, the connection with this parliament disappeared, and we believe it is important to shield it and strengthen it. I would also like to stress the acceptance of Amendment No. 3 to highlight the public character of the Greek statistical system, which, with the wish of the People's Party for the privatization of our instruments, is never too much. We regret, however, that Amendment No 12 is not accepted, for example, that it seeks to give priority to the application of this plan in the execution of programmes in the public domain, and that when it is not done it is subject to the deliberations and consideration of the Galego Statistical Council. Others of the amendments accepted, such as 2, 4 or 11, give more clarity to the text and we understand that it also reinforces the socialization of statistical work. But we have to say that, in general, the main objection we have to the final outcome, which, except for surprises, we will see today, is the People's Party's refusal to approve those amendments which precisely put the focus beyond what is already being done and which could entail qualitative progress or also the commitment to statistics that we have judged absolutely necessary in this new context. For example, we regret that in Article 8, which is where quality objectives are arbitrated, there are no two proposals that will make us incorporate both the perspective of gender and social knowledge. First of all, the perspective of gender as a measurement also of the quality of statistical work, because we understand that we need to commit ourselves to moving methodologically forward in measuring the impact of gender and the different realities as well as of gender and gender, which is something that the same equality report made by the competent board warned of the difficulties in this area, the shadows that exist and that have to sit down to analyze them. We wonder why not be vanguarded in analyzing this reality as well. We learn from feminism that what is not named does not exist, nor does what is not measured. Secondly, regarding social knowledge, we understand that we are at a time when we have to get out of Excel to the street and move the knowledge that is generated at the Galego Institute of Statistics with more momentum towards society. To that end, we propose something as absolutely revolutionary as the media and social media are improved, the dissemination campaigns are carried out, and there is greater collaboration with entities that can disseminate statistical work. But the majority group says that the perspective of gender and social knowledge are not an element of quality, and from then on we disagree, we understand that these are areas that will remain lame. In the same way, we regret that there are, in particular, two additional provisions that we propose from the Galician Nationalist Block that are not accepted, and we intend with them to shield the commitment to two statistics or to confront them in this economic context, where we know, in addition, that we are the women who are the most going to pay the pandemic of coward-19, which are fundamental and precisely deepen on that historical line that we have not been women a priority for statistical science either. First of all, this is the case with the satellite household production account, which we have already brought to this parliament on other occasions. It is unacceptable that since 2013 we have not had a measurement by the Galego Institute of Statistics of how much in our economy the invisible work of care, the invisible work of the home. The last estimate — I want to remind you that you have data 11 years ago, which was published in 2013 even though the data are 11 years ago — told us that it would be about 50.6% of our country's gross domestic product. We insist that this should be a priority. Apologies that we need to adapt to the European state calendar or that are very expensive seem to us to be insufficient. What we say is that if the state and Europe go slow on this issue and it is not a priority, we exercise our self-government. Let's say that for Galicia if it is a priority to study this area, and therefore instead of putting so many ties and candles on the 40th anniversary of the Statute, we use one of the little powers that gives us and say that if we want to measure the value of care, because without care there is no life. [[Applause]] Secondly, I would also like to mention that we propose to periodically stabilize within the public system the macro-enquisition of protection over gender violence. The Board, after several years of negatives, has recently drawn up one again; it has done so with private media, not even hung up the entire report on the website, the entire documentation, its summary of results, and, of course, we understand that it is deeply scientific and sterile, which gives for a press round of the advisor but little more. What we have to encourage is the comparability of data and therefore it should be consolidated and professionalized, and for that we have the Galego Institute of Statistics, where, moreover, it is also introduced, if other data from the Autonomous Community are compared, such as the number of aids, its volume, etc. So we don't understand this refusal, and we also warn that it is a missed opportunity for this country to continue to move forward in favour of the fight against masculine violence, and, in addition, doing so in an advanced way with respect to other territories. Finally, I would also like to make a brief mention of the additions that we have incorporated into the annexes, that section of the law where information goals appear, and where we basically list absences that we understand are serious and unjustifiable in the Greek statistical field. We accept, through four transactions, issues that seem important to us and will be incorporated specifically: the study of water prices; housing statistics will be improved; housing statistics will be incorporated or maintained — better said — the area of aquaculture, which disappeared from the initial project; and there seems to be also a commitment to study the area of energy prices, which is one of the most important economic problems for homes and for small and medium-sized enterprises in this country. However, we understand that, nevertheless, despite this acceptance of these four areas, it adds to the absences, and I would like to point out some as well. A reflection that was already taking place at the beginning of my intervention: we are in a pandemic and, of course, it gives the feeling, seeing this law, that the pandemic exists but it's like water and oil with respect to the Galego Institute of Statistics. The fact is, of course, that the statistics we already have provide information on the impact of the pandemic, but we understand that we would have to incorporate in a transitional way specific goals that allow us to make an analysis of the impact of covid-19 in a broader way in areas such as the education system and the health system and the impact on the health of the population, on the market itself, on working conditions, on social conditions and living materials, for example, specific statistics for a specific historical moment. From our point of view, we also lack goals that enable us to meet the challenges that we have ahead. That is why we insist on the start of this intervention in the reflection on anticipation. So, there are areas such as mobility and energy transition, the practical needs and costs of this area, or the research and innovation transfer in the productive fabric of basic research that we understand are elements in which this country has to wager, and that, if we do not measure how we are, we can hardly hit the path to where we are going. And I would also like to make a reflection on the absence of these information goals in the field of equality. We, of course, started that we will be a more feminist Galician if we also intervene with a real knowledge of what we have in the country. In that sense, we propose that we incorporate, for example, a survey of the reasons for motherhood and paternity, and that we know what really leads people in this country to because they have no children, because there is a concern on the part of the People's Parliamentary Group and the party that supports the Government to permanently hold us responsible for women, which is also good that in this country if men were asked, in general, to all people, why don't they make that decision, because then we had some surprise. [[Applause]] In this same sense, we understand that there are realities that we cannot close our eyes by a conception too crossed by the Catholicism of this country. On the one hand, everything that has to do with trans reality and, on the other hand, everything that has to do with selected monoparentity. In that sense, in addition to deepening this statistic, we also intend that in our amendments this blocking situation of the Galego Register of Single-parent Families, which has not only been developed by the Board for ten years, but that in its own Demographic Impulse Law they would re-enter that measure and already comply with the deadline they enabled. We therefore regret that this matter is not dealt with either. Finally, to point out that for us there are no targets to address and to be able to democratically exercise the rights of our analysis, as I say, to deal specifically with the process of privatization and dismantling of the public system. And I want to set examples. We have recently seen this demoler report from the Board of Auditors telling us that since 2009 you have already cut around two thousand five hundred teachers and teachers of public education; the Board of Auditors says so, does not tell the BNG; and of course, from our point of view, that explains that you do not want to incorporate more comprehensive goals to address, for example, the direct and indirect transfer of public spending to private education, or the loss of teachers or other elements such as ratios. Again, we have examples of issues that hide this parliament, like everything that refers to ratios in urban contexts. In any case, serve this example. We also propose specific goals for health and public service, etc., to put a question into context. We use the technical argument that they are too specific targets and under the umbrella of broader goals that could already be done by these statistics, but from then on it covers the background political position, which are areas that are really not being studied, and so we reflect on it in this way in the debate. In conclusion, to finish, as I was beginning, from the BNG we believe in what Toffler said that knowledge is the most democratic source of power. That's why we understand that in order to know statistical science, this new plan is fundamental. We do not want to deny it: it brings up important issues for the collective knowledge of the country, but we understand that we also miss an opportunity to jump forward, a qualitative leap in a historical context that will demand us faster, more efficiency and more anticipation. So our sense of vote will be, if there is no change in that sense, that of abstention. Nothing more and thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u8	Thank you, Mrs. Presas. [[One person from the House's cleaning services comes to disinfect the floor.]] Popular Parliamentary Group, Mrs Sanz.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u9	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies. Let me begin my speech with a quote from British physicist and mathematician William Thomson that says that what is not measured cannot be improved and what is not improved is always degraded. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are debating the bill of the Greek Statistical Plan for the five-year period 2022-2026. It was in 1997 when the first Statistical Plan was adopted in this parliament, and today, twenty-four years later, we are debating the content of the sixth plan, with the aim of consolidating and expanding the achievements achieved in the five previous plans and with the objective of planning, obtaining and disseminating information of interest to Galicia. We're talking about statistics, and it's not usual that this is a matter of parliamentary debate in itself, when the reality is that all the rest of the interventions, initiatives, bills, government control actions, ultimately, our daily work as parliamentarians is based on statistical information. For that reason, I would like to see the short time we spend today in this science serve at least to give us a joint reflection on the importance of public statistical activity and also its link with democracy. The image, the picture of the reality that the statistics offer, should serve as a tool for governments to make appropriate political decisions, but also for every citizen, every one of the Greeks to be informed about the outcome of these policies, whether or not they get the different administrations. It's true that data doesn't change the world in itself, but it makes that change possible, identifying needs and measuring progress. We need quality data, reliable and contrasting, in a world that is characterized, not by fake news, by a growing trend towards instant information, whose quality is unaccredited. If, in addition to being of quality, these data are accessible, as is the case with the information provided by the Galego Institute of Statistics, these data enable citizens to make changes, activate auditing accounts, and are -- it should be emphasized today -- an exercise of transparency. Finally, we must see official statistical production as a public good, as an essential public good for governance in democratic societies and also as a public good for the citizen's control of Government action. In Galicia we are heirs of the statutory forecast that has made statistics an exclusive competence of the Autonomous Community. The drafters of the Autonomy Statute made it clear that the information provided to us from the Central Administration would not be sufficient for the exercise of good self-government and included in Article 27.6 the exclusive competence of the statistics of Greek interest. The first stone was thus placed in the construction of an own Greek statistical system, which was further consolidated from 1988 with the approval of the Greek Statistics Act. However, Galicia already had previous examples of statistical uncertainty, such as the Regional Observatory created by Professor Bernardo Pena Trapero in the 1970s or with the creation of the Galician Statistical Information Centre in 1984. With the approval of the law now in force, the competence collected in the Statute is developed, the obligation is laid down to plan and measure the results achieved and the statistical organisation of the Autonomous Community is configured. The Galego Institute of Statistics, the statistical bodies of the councils and the Galego Council of Statistics are thus created. The IGE is the central part of the statistical organization, and its main function is to direct and coordinate Galicia's statistical activity. The statistical bodies of the council, for their part, run the statistics on their scope of action; and in this debate we must remember the importance of these bodies, those of the councils, within the Greek statistical realization, since they are usually not put into value, since they do a work as it is not visible to society, when reality is that their contribution to the Greek public statistics is essential. And finally, the Galego Statistical Council, as a body for the participation of social agents, where business, trade union, professional organizations, universities, local administration and since 2011, and on the initiative of the People's Government, also parliamentary groups. While at the time of the creation of the IGE, it was affixed by its own production, with Parliament's approval of the first plan in the period 1998-2001, it was a qualitative leap. Since then, decisions on statistical matters depend not only on the political impulse of the Government that corresponds to them, but on a rule of law approved by the representatives of the citizen, before which accounts must be held. During this period, official statistics were growing, improving and expanding the demographic, social, economic, cultural and environmental information of Galicia. It was a stage of major changes in the ways of doing, measuring reality and disseminating the results of statistics. And today, after more than thirty-three years of activity, I believe that there is a broad consensus — and so it was expressed in the previous speeches by the spokesmen — that the IGE has reached a high level of prestige, independence, professionalism and usefulness for the whole of Galician society. It's one of the most established autonomous institutions that has allowed us to make autonomous politics and defend our self-government. If we remember, we will remember how the IGE has always relied on the support of the People's Parliamentary Group, especially at a delicate time of its trajectory, as when after the publication of the Report for the Reform of Public Administrations — known as the Cora Report — it has in some way questioned the need for a decentralized statistical system, or how when in the processes of rationalization of the self-government public sector itself it has come to formulate its possible fusion with other autonomous entities. At that time, this parliamentary group — the People's Group — supported those proposals that made a savings to public buildings without losing their effectiveness. But — and here is the important thing — he did not accept any measure affecting or cutting our Community's self-government institutions. Moreover, with this objective we advocate the need to maintain and consolidate institutions such as Meteogalicia, the People's Value, the Board of Auditors or the Advisory Council, and of course — and as I have already indicated — the continuity and independence of the Galego Institute of Statistics. And we want to put that fact in value this very year in which we have attained the 40th anniversary of the Statute, because the defense of self-government also goes through the defense of our autonomous institutions. And now that we celebrate this 40th anniversary, it's a pity not all political groups value what I have proposed for the development of Autonomy the approval of the Statute and the creation of such entities as the IGE. Ladies and gentlemen, today's plan seeks to create and disseminate statistical knowledge for the next five years, meets the current needs of citizens, public and private institutions, and consolidates and expands the objectives and methods achieved in previous plans. I recall again in this plenary our unforgettable Valeriano Martínez when the presentation at the Galego Statistical Council of this plan underlined that this plan will deepen in transparency, quality and accessibility of information. In this line, the new plan expressly assumes the code of conduct of good European practices, and this fact will assume that our statistical organisation will be subject to its principles and the Greek Government will promote and respect them; principles such as the professional independence of statistical staff, impartiality, objectivity of information, effectiveness, quality and transparency. [[Applause]] Another objective for the new plan will be to integrate administrative information into statistical processes, so that citizens are not to be asked for that information already in the hands of the Administration. This procedure will prevent unnecessary surveys, which are always annoying, costs will be reduced and data will be provided in time and form necessary for decision-making. Ladies and gentlemen, the pandemic has left us many lessons and, among them, the need for statistical information that comes in time to make the appropriate decisions; and with this new way of working one seeks to achieve that objective. Similarly, the new plan will strengthen interoperability between administrations in order to increase their efficiency. Let us remember that the IGE not only works, but also in collaboration with the European statistical system — which is Eurostat —, with the national — the INE — or with the northern one of Portugal — as pioneers in giving harmonised data on a euro region. The relationship of the IGE with other systems, in particular INE, has always been presided over by loyalty and non-duality; an idea which affects the presentation of reasons for this plan, which maintains the principle of efficient use of public resources. And there is the definition of own statistics collected in the text as the one executed to expand the information on Galicia provided by other statistical systems. Thus, the IGE takes advantage of all existing information, seeing the above-mentioned Eurostat and INE, but also other sources, such as the Tax Agenda, Social Security or universities, and uses the available resources to expand this information. They also pursue the new plan improvements in the accessibility of information, the dissemination of information through reusable formats, so that they can work more easily on it, and at this point I have to value the IGE website. Since its launch, this website has been incorporating technological advances, such as multitables, graphs and measurement maps or APIs, which has made it, more than simply a broadcast tool, into a customization and information retrieval application. The improvement is continuous and the IGE continues to work on the accessibility of its website, for example today to allow it to be displayed from mobile phones. But there is no doubt that it has already become a very good and useful tool for society, as believed by the 40,000 average visits it receives a month or the twenty million information it receives a year. Another novelty of the plan will be the generation of information related to the challenges of the Strategic Plan Galicia 2021-2030, with the objective of sustainable development of Agenda 2020-2030 or — very important — with the monitoring and evaluation of public policies. And to finish, highlight as a relevant aspect of the plan the introduction of the age perspective to address the demographic decline. Including for the first time in Galicia's statistical planning this perspective will involve an extra effort to the statistical system but will favor demographic analysis and decision-making in this area. It is summed up in the perspective of gender, which was already present in the statistics of Greece since the adoption of Law 7/2004, of equality of women and men. And I use it to remember at this point that Galicia was a pioneer in gender considerations in the surveys, since at the state level this obligation was not settled until three years later. I've concluded this first shift, ladies and gentlemen. This plan, which we are expected to adopt today, is for everyone and for all. It is from this parliament, where we are debating it today, where we will make use of the outcome of its work continuously in our debates and where we will be aware of its evaluation once it has been completed. It's from the IGE itself and the statistical bodies of the councils, who will execute it. It's from the German Government, which will develop it through the annual programs. And above all, it's from the Greeks and the Greeks, because having a good public statistical system like ours will allow the self-government to succeed and take the proper course of the country and succeed in making political decisions that affect citizens. Nothing else on this first shift, thank you very much.

The goal was to reduce the average of the total burnt area as compared to the average of the last ten years, which is 19,986 hectares. I mean, three times better than the average of the last ten years. Another important goal is the surface burned by fire. Well, in this case, we've reached 81.55% of fires below a hectare. And speaking of figures, I also give you the investment figure of this agreement since it started: almost 29 million euros. I remind you that the Convention, in addition to the creation of a public service for cleaning secondary fringes for priority parishes - I will refer to these priority parishes - has a number of features for the 273 municipalities, for all those who are affiliated, for example, the Xesbius application, which allows them to manage all the administrative processing of the strips and provides them with a view to knowing what those fringes are. As you know, the Convention places special emphasis on priority parishes, 157 parishes of 81 municipalities determined by technical criteria. And in these parishes there is a public service for cleaning the fringes, by the public authority Seaga, for a price of EUR 350 per hectare, a price significantly lower than the market price. In addition, Seaga's prevention supervisors are those who check the state of the fringes and assume all the processing of the subsidiary executions in case the owner does not carry out the cleaning of his estates within a period marked by law. I mean, we cooperate with the municipalities in this work, which, according to Law 3/2007, is municipal jurisdiction. Well, in these parishes, in the 157 priorities, ladies and gentlemen, we have the complete data and we can ensure that the Convention is fulfilling its objectives, because since its establishment, the percentage of the surface managed by its owners or by Seaga with the public cleaning service has gone from being just over 50% of the surface to almost 70% of this 2021. I'll also give them the total data of these 92 municipalities. Look, the people who work in this field have so far checked the state of 685,000 plots, which account for 4400 hectares. Of the 44,000 hectares, the surface percentage managed — and this is very good data — by their owners is 65%; that is, 29,000 hectares distributed on 432,000 plots. And the unmanaged hectares of the 44,000 are 15,000 and account for 253,000 plots. The owners of these unmanaged parcels are subject to the corresponding notification, cautioning them that, in case they do not clean the two farms, the public administration will carry out such cleaning, with the consequent impact of the cost and the imposition of the corresponding penalty. In case the owner of the plot is not located - and, ladies, this is a complicated issue and we are seeing that there are many plots in which we do not locate the owner - it is being published in the corresponding official newspapers. Also with data on November 30, we have generated in the scope of the Convention a total of 35,326 notifications and 33,129 publications; that is, practically, 68,000 between notifications and publications. Also this year, 2021, as an improvement of the Convention, decided to deploy a pilot into eight municipalities, selected with technical criteria, in which the cleaning and disposal work of prohibited tree species in secondary strips is involved. In this case, these jobs have been taking place since November by employees of the Seaga public enterprise and also by staff of the Galician Forest Fire Prevention and Defense Service. I repeat my gratitude and willingness and willingness to do these preventive tasks again. These works we are going to extend to four other municipalities to have a pilot before the next summer of twelve selected municipalities, as I say, based on technical criteria and fire occurrence, which have completely cleaned their fringes, completely managed, as I say, before the most risky season of the year 2022. So I spoke, as I told you, in this third section of my appearance, about prevention and the Convention, and I would like to end by referring also to the new comprehensive forest fire fighting law. In this area, taking up what has been said in this Parliament, one of the main conclusions of the 2018 study committee was the need to develop a new law on the prevention and extinction of forest fires that would collect the best of previous legislation. And I would like to remind you that the Galician Fire Prevention and Extinction Act is from 2007, Law 3/2007, but it has also incorporated the forecasts that emanated from the dictates themselves; I repeat, recommendations that come from the agreement of the three fourths of this Parliament. In addition, we had to incorporate - if he told us - the result of the report by the group of experts who were appointed by the political parties representing in Parliament as regards recommendation No. 60 of the dictation. This report was issued in July 2019. And with this starting point, the work of drafting guidelines for the drafting of the draft comprehensive fight law against Galician forest fires has been addressed. For this reason, for the elaboration of the guidelines, it has been configured - it is being configured - a participatory process, coordinated by the University of Vigo, under the direction of two people of recognized prestige in this area, such as Juan Picos and José Antonio Vega, to whom I want to make an express recognition and thank them for their commitment and involvement in a topic of so much transcendence to Galicia as the fight against forest fires. Ladies and gentlemen, in this participatory process more than 200 people have been involved in representing all entities, bodies and collectives that are directly or indirectly related to the Greek forestry activity. I would like to point out that there is an important involvement of personnel in the fire prevention and extinguishing device, as it could not be otherwise. And as a result of this joint work, we have guidelines that serve us as a starting point for the drafting of the draft law; guidelines that will be presented, as cannot be otherwise, to the Forest Council next January 11th, as a step before the beginning of the preliminary draft process, which will enter this Parliament - insurance - before the end of 2022. Ladies and gentlemen, I want to make it clear that the fact that you don't have a new law until next year doesn't mean that we'll stay still as far as the fight against fires is concerned; that's what I'm explaining to you in this audience. But of course, the new law has to move forward in matters that have so far been unresolved, and I am going to state some of them which I think are important and which I would like to introduce in the debate of this audience. I repeat that the guidelines are not a question of the Rural Council, but they are the result of a participatory process. They are moving forward in matters such as the division of competences and cooperation mechanisms. The guidelines assess the development we have already made in 2018 of the General Directorate for Forest Planning and Management and the Defence of the Mountain, but they also focus on the important role that the Galician Rural Development Agency has to play as the manager of the Land Recovery Act. With regard to the competition distribution, starting - and thus recognised both in the Committee's opinion and also in the expert report - that the main competence in this matter reaches the Autonomous Community, the role assumed by the other administrations must be clear. The guidelines say that there is a need for improved administrative cooperation, especially in the area of prevention, in particular with regard to local administration, indicating that it is necessary to follow the line of cooperation with the Convention on the Protection of Villages. But it also includes, in local administrations, deputies. Ladies and gentlemen, it is not to be welcomed that only tractors are currently being delivered for prevention to the municipalities of Ourense province, because the rest of the Members refuse to collaborate in the financing of these machinery with the Council of Galicia. The guidelines also define the comprehensive forest fire control system, which covers three areas, from a conceptual point of view: the preparation, prevention and anticipation of the forest fire problem, distinguishing between preventive planning and what is strictly work or prevention tasks; the extinction of forest fires; and the restoration and recovery of land affected by fires. And he also says that the German comprehensive forest fire fighting system must be based on a professionalized structure that must have an integrated territorial basis under the sole command of the forest administration, which I'm sure we all agree on. It must be a fire device with adequate training and preparation at all times. I would like to remind you that in the budgets of the year 2022 we have EUR 500,000 for staff training; an autonomous device, ladies and gentlemen, that needs to be complemented - and this is seen by the report of experts designated by the parliamentary groups of the year 2019 - with new municipal brigades that will have as their primary object monitoring and prevention, together with support for the device in logistics issues and collaboration in the liquidation of stabilized or controlled fires or even in the extinction of low intensity fires; professionalised municipal brigades, with a uniform selection mechanism at the autonomous level and with a minimum recruitment time of six months; and again this proposal also requires the financial involvement of provincial deputies. In the new law we have to clarify the importance of monitoring. The guidelines also support the creation of that unit of fifteen agents exclusively dedicated to research tasks. We are also talking, of course, about extinction, and in this sense, the guidelines point to the need to set up specific specialized equipment for intervention in large fires - you know that they are over 500 hectares - as well as in their subsequent analysis, and on an important issue - in which we are already working, we are going to see results in 2022 - in a more detailed definition of the figure of the Technical Director of Extinction and the need to have a support technical team, analysts. The law must also recognize the fundamental role, as I said before, of these specific brigades dedicated to research. And in addition, experts conclude, in the field of prevention, that any action that takes place in the territory of Galicia must always be permeated by the concept of anticipation in the face of forest fires. The key to anticipation, ladies and gentlemen, is, without a doubt, the management of the territory. The results in the medium and long term will come from that territory management. And in this area, the guidelines propose that two concepts which seem particularly important to me are defined in the law and which I would like to highlight. First of all, the cutting-edge landscapes, looking for a generation of decontinuity in biomass as a preventive mechanism that must have a productive vocation for them to be sustainable over time. And it distinguishes four types of landscape cutoffs: the cut-off polygons of the Land Recovery Act; the joint management forest groupings, which manage the territory with prevention criteria; the biomass management fringe networks, collected in Law 3/2007; and also, finally, the specific areas of the territory which do not have the condition of fringe networks and which necessarily have to be managed to prevent fires or the worsening of their consequences. Here, in the guidelines, we are even asked that there may be direct management by the Administration in that kind of part of the territory. And another concept that needs to be incorporated into the law, according to the guidelines -- and we also agree -- is that of mosaic landscape, so traditional and connected to the Greek territory. The promotion of mosaic landscape, along with the implementation of the Recovery Act, will reduce the risk of major fires in areas with high degree of abandonment of primary activity. It also involves — and of course — the need to maintain primary and secondary biomass management fringes, although we will have to evolve them with these years' experience. I think this is a subject that we should all debate: how can we evolve the operation of these strips, taking into account those figures that I spoke to them before what we understand are the fringes, both in numbers of hectares and plots. Special concern is expressed by the experts who have drawn up these guidelines from the secondary fringes around the villages, where it is necessary - we say so, and we are already moving it forward with the model villages - from the annual detour to the promotion of agricultural activity or the deployment of leafy plants as a sustainable mechanism for the protection of villages from forest fires. The cost of unlocking 180,000 or 230,000 hectares every year, as I told them before, is inescapable. The guidelines also contain a specific section aimed at raising awareness and raising civil society's awareness of the problem of forest fires and incendiary fires. They concern the importance of performing simulations in the territory that improve prevention measures or self-protection actions of the population. This will start in the same year 2022, at the 2022 Pladigo, precisely by seeking self-protection, especially in villages with the greatest risk of fires. I finish this first part of my appearance trusting you to listen to what you have to say, because, as always, my will, in addition to moving -- which is what I've come to do here today -- information on the fire campaign and taking into account the actions of the Government of Greece, is also to listen and, precisely, to seek consensuss that can benefit our citizens. I think, ladies and gentlemen - and I sincerely believe - that the parliamentary process of the new comprehensive forest fire-fighting law can be the ideal framework for all parliamentary groups to come to an agreement, to a community pact, to carry out a joint strategy in the fight against forest fires, against that scourge that afflicts our country every year, against the criminals who burn Galicia. And I ask you to face all of us together, Government, parliamentary groups, experts, members of the service, all who have taken part in the process of drafting the guidelines, the challenges that this struggle entails, and that we have among us all enough time to carry out the mandate given to us by the citizens who voted for us: to agree on the solution of their problems. Nothing else and thank you very much.

G.P. motion. of the Socialists of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr Martín Seco García, on the policy of the Government of Greece in respect of the beef sector
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u53	No amendments were tabled. Mr. Seco has the floor to formulate the motion.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u54	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party, the productive sectors of Galicia are having a very hard time. The dairy sector is at war in the streets, with producers fighting for a fair price for their milk. But if this situation is worrying, with companies that do not guarantee contracts with prices that cover their production costs, we must not forget a sector that is going through much worse than milk and with less capacity for mobilization and display, which is the meat sector. Producers who are launching an emergency message in the face of the possibility of the disappearance of many small farms that are sweeping our rural territory; a message that, unfortunately, is not silent in the German Government. In the last seventeen years, the price that livestock perceive for the meat they produce has increased by only 12% and, on the other hand, in that period, the CPI has experienced an increase of 33% and the production costs of the farms have risen by no more than 38%. It is only necessary to make figures to see that this situation is jeopardizing the economic viability of farms that are essential for Galicia, because, in addition to producing meat of high quality under strict parameters of animal welfare and also environmental sustainability, they play an indispensable role from the point of view of land management, the fight against the abandonment of rural areas and the fight against forest fires, because, gentlemen of the People's Party, as the councillor said, there is no better measure against forest fires than the management of our territory and the placing in value of our land. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u61	Thank you, Mr. Martín Seco. [[One person from the House's cleaning services comes to disinfect the floor.]] Motion by G. P. of the Galego Nationalist Block, at the initiative of Mr. Manuel Antonio Lourenzo Sobral, on the policy of the Government of Galicia in relation to the situation of the Greek language in teaching
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u62	No amendments were tabled. To formulate the motion, Mr. Lourenzo Sobral has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u63	Thank you, President. In 2005, Kiri Davis, an African American student from New York, published documentary A girl like me. In this documentary in just seven minutes he approached and repeated an experiment that had already been carried out by psychologists, Afro-Americans as well, Kenneth Bancroft Clark and Mamie Phipps Clark in the 1940s. What was this experiment? Well basically in which African American children and children had to choose which dolls they wanted to play with. They were identical dolls, with a single difference: that dolls were white and other dolls represented black dolls. The result of this work by Kiri Davis -- which I'm sure many of the ones we know here -- because there was a video a few years ago that swept through many educational centers in our country by making the relationship between what was linguistic prejudice and racial prejudice -- drew the attention of the entire international community and put three reflections on the table that we believe is important to share here today among all of us. The first of this reflection is that, after decades of a priori policies designed to combat linguistic prejudice, the results obtained in Kiri Davis' work were practically the same as those obtained by Clark's marriage in 1940. That is, 75% of African American children and girls chose to play white dolls and maintained the prejudice of associating white dolls with beautiful, good, and black dolls with ugly and bad; it was an unconscious prejudice. The next of these learnings and reflections that you put on the table is that this choice can be called anyway, except freedom. And the third of these reflections that are put on the table — and that directly fits into the debate that we have here today — is that prejudices are a social scourge that we must exterminate and that prejudice has both to be protected from skin color, sexual orientation or the language in which we speak. Prejudice is a scourge and it is something that we must therefore exterminate from this society. [[Applause]] And, therefore, the question we want to bring here, which we want to share, is whether someone would think it would be normal for, for example, Kiri Davis or Clark's marriage to say to the results of that study that this was a question of freedom, that the children freely chose which dolls they played, that there was nothing under that choice; if anyone would understand that on the basis of those results that were given in American society at that time because governments did not act in the educational system, for example — among other areas — to cut this situation. I think everyone would understand that if these steps were not taken, if they were to perpetuate the prejudice of supremacism. Well, ladies and gentlemen of the People's Party, this is exactly what the Government of Galicia is doing in addressing language prejudices in our country. And we put the data on the table. So, as was the case in the selection of white dolls and black dolls, the IGE data -- the IGE, not the BNG -- says the following: eight out of ten children in Greece selects Spanish to navigate the Internet or social networks; only 6% of the children in Greece picks books in English, compared to 94% that selects books in Spanish; only two out of ten children in Greece selects Greek for television, for music, etc. I don't even take a comma from the IGE data or the reality they're reflecting the Galician board's own studies. What if I think it deserves a reflection, just like Clark's marriage, like Kiri Davis did, is that it's legitimate that the Education, Culture and University Advisor Roman Rodríguez said in this plenary just two weeks ago: "we have two languages that we use in absolute freedom." "In the street, normality, cordiality and the spirit of our culture prevails." ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u79	Thank you very much, President. Good afternoon. In the year 2000 — first of the Fexóo era — Galicia had 13,349 dairy farms; ten years later, in 2019, we would have fallen to 7,688 and two years later, in this 2021 we are walking, we are in 6,443; that is, in twelve years we lost 6,906 dairy farms, an average of 575 farms per year. And of no use was the so-called "rural legislation", which began in 2016. It must be said — if any — that these figures do not produce them by the alarmists, the BNG catastrophes; the catastrophe in this case has a name: the People's Party. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u97	To formulate the non-law proposition Mr. Bará has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2021-12-09-DSPG060.u98	Good afternoon. I would like to begin with an initial clarification of this proposal, which is not a generic initiative on the improvement of the railway services which have been abolished because of the pandemic, nor is it an initiative for the improvement of the railway infrastructures that Galicia needs, which are, as we know, many, because on these matters there have already been unanimous agreements in this Parliament, some of them very recent; and I am referring, for example, to an agreement from the 2nd committee last November and also to an agreement in plenary in the debate on the state of the nation, where there has also been a unanimous agreement on these issues. And it's not a non-legislative proposal about the time changes caused by the new services of AVE, which, as we know, is a very serious matter, which will cause major changes in hours and will harm people who use the railway in the cities of Greece to benefit the users of the train to Madrid. This, of course, is a scandal to us, and the BNG has already put forward initiatives in both the state Parliament and in this Parliament which we will be debating in the near future, where we will have the opportunity to stand on this aggravation, as well as on prices. What we want today is to focus the issue on another area. We want to give a substantive debate on infrastructure and mobility competences and to pay attention to what seems to us to be a great anomaly, which, in addition, does not correspond to what you say is the most decentralized state in Europe. This anomaly is that Galicia has no competence in most of the infrastructures and mobility systems: we have no competence in the large ports called general interest of the State, airports, roads and highways and neither on the railway. This is what we mean when we talk about thinking about Galicia in a small way, thinking about Galicia as a kind of MP who manages roads and public transport in front of the idea of thinking Galicia in great terms, spreading all the potential and exercising all the competences in infrastructure and mobility. And that's exactly what the People's Party doesn't want -- according to you -- not to break Spain and also not to disturb their bosses in Madrid, who have the command at a distance and press it when you have to press the button. We therefore want to give a substantive debate on rail as a mode of mobility for the future, of the twenty-first century, thought, in addition to the interests of the country, both for people and for commodities, and fulfilling three priority functions: a mode of sustainable mobility, fundamental now, in addition to the debate on environmental emergency and climate change; a means of fast and secure connectivity between the main urban areas and the villages of the country; and, thirdly, an element of vertebration and territorial cohesion. In this sense, to give this debate, we have a big problem, which is that neither the Central Government nor the Union - and this is serious, in addition to the case of the Greek Government - they have strategies, no plans, no political priorities in relation to the German railway. And one thing happens, that by the absence and incomparency, in this case, of the Government of the United States, who has to act as the Government of the United States, making strategies and plans is the Atlantic axis. This is so, and I have many documents here that believe it: Documents from the Atlantic Axis: "Report on the dry port of Monforte de Lemos", of the year 2015, "Report on the railway in the province of Lugo", with all the demands concerning the communications of Lugo with the rest of the territory of Galicia and with the rest of the state and with Portugal, "Elements for the drafting of a plan director of the Atlantic corridor", a document that should be made by the Central Government or, in its case, by the Unity of Galicia, and that had to do the Atlantic axis. [[Mr. Bará Torres shows various documents to the members of the House.]] Or what we can consider is a genuine White Paper of all the needs that Galicia currently has in rail matters, which is the Railway Map of Galicia, a map of the actions proposed by the Atlantic axis for the modernisation of Galicia's wide rail network for both people and commodities. This is reality, and who is doing the role the Government should play is an entity like the Atlantic Axis. This is a lot to think about, and they should worry about not exercising their powers. [[Applause]] These reports, these studies, are fundamental for us to have a model of our own German train for the 21st century, which is already being started, with two decades; a model that enables you to overcome that which you defend -- or at least it looks like -- which is the radial model of the AVE, which the one that benefits -- is very clear -- is in the center and Madrid. It's the model of "madricentrism." In front of that, we need a model of our own: a German railway with improvements in infrastructures, some of them that have to be necessarily new construction, and better services as compared to times and frequencies. And that's why we bring here two strategic proposals for mobility for both people and commodities. We're going to focus on people, even though commodities, with the debate in the state, too, with the priorities in the state, would give rise to a monographic debate of great interest. But today, we are going to focus on people's mobility, and we have two concrete proposals: the first is the creation of the proximity railway service and the transfer of this service and these infrastructure to Galicia, and the second is the creation of the German railway body, which we also name, we call it "Galtrén." If they don't like you, we can discuss another, but at least we'll name it. With regard to the first question, the proximity train must be a priority in internal mobility in urban areas for labour, education, services or leisure reasons, which is why most people move all the time during the week, not because of going to Madrid, that too; of course, it is a right that we have to go by train, but it is not the priority need of the day-to-day population in their mobility. This is, in particular, a priority that you have recently shown and made Portugal public when you talked about making a mesh throughout the territory to connect the towns of over 20,000 inhabitants with proximity services. It's urgent to end this anomaly, which is another real anomaly that Galicia is one of the few communities in the Spanish State that has no nearby railway service. I have here the map of the vicinity in the state. They have nearby Asturias, Cantabria, Euskadi, Catalonia, Valencian Community, Murcia, Andalusia and Madrid. [[Mr. Bará Torres shows a map to House members.]] Galicia is off the map of proximity and this is not normal. If we compare ourselves to Asturias, which is next door and has much less population density, it has nearby services; it has 500 trains a day, which are four times more than the trains that Galicia has. And we could compare, for example, the railway services between Oviedo and Avilés, which are 32 a week, and those that are, for example, between Ferrol and La Coruña, which have no comparison point, with 4 a day. To this you must add, in addition to higher frequencies, the advantages of prices, which are, of course, much lower in the case of the proximity train, or the single ticket, which means that one can move in Asturias with a single ticket in all means of transport. That's the real intermodality, and don't do two train and bus stations, one side to the other. That's not for intermodality at all. And secondly, there's the proposal of the German railway force, which is neither new nor new, because you've defended it here repeatedly. It has already reached an agreement in September 2009 in this Parliament to create this railway body, announced in November 2009 at a meeting of the so-called councillor, who spoke of the creation of the railway public for the railway management of the vicinity in Galicia or when the board contracted the contract for the creation of the German railway body in 2010, which later remained in nothing, remained on wet paper, because you did not really wager by this system, because you would tell him in Madrid that there was no need to go there, and we have already seen where they were after Madrid. [[Mr. Bará Torres shows a document.]] It was, from 2012, with a genuine dismantling and with an annihilation of much of the German internal railway system, that we will then discuss in more detail. And with regard to this proposal, we introduced a self-amendment, because it contained a deadline in the initial wording of 2021 which does not seem to us to be assumed at this time of year. That's why we included a deadline of the first half of the year 2022 to bid this project for the creation of the German railway force. I'm finishing by saying what are the fundamental objectives of this initiative, which is the impulse of a German train for the 21st century to improve infrastructure, services, more often, better times and cheaper prices, so that we have the railway management from Galicia, as provided for in the Statute in its article 27, and so that we actually make a strategic bet by the train so that this is the decade of the German train. Thank you very much.
